I just kind of get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes start, do you notice any strange physical sensations, like an unusual smell, a rising feeling in your stomach, or a sudden sense of déjà vu?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't smelled anything weird or felt anything like that in my stomach. And it's definitely not déjà vu. I don't really know why I was doing it, but it freaked my coworkers out, so... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: When you 'freeze up' or act this way, do you have any memory of what happened during those moments, or is there a gap in your memory afterward?\n\nPatient: No, I remember it. It's not like I black out or anything. I know I'm standing there, and I can hear myself saying the numbers. I just... I can't really seem to snap out of it or move on to the next task. So, yeah, there's no gap in my memory. I just kind of get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes start, do you notice any strange physical sensations, like an unusual smell, a rising feeling in your stomach, or a sudden sense of déjà vu?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't smelled anything weird or felt anything like that in my stomach. And it's definitely not déjà vu. It just kind of happens, without any warning signs like that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of these specific episodes at work, do you find yourself feeling constantly worried or anxious about various aspects of your life, like your health, finances, or other daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about normal things sometimes, like paying rent, but it's not constant. I'm not up at night stressing about my health or anything like that. It's just the stuff at work that's really bothering me right now.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you notice any repetitive physical movements, such as picking at your clothes, smacking your lips, or twitching in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do any of that. I'm not... I don't pick at my clothes or smack my lips or anything. I really just stand there still while I'm stuck on the numbers. So, no, no twitching or weird movements like that.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you feel 'stuck,' do you feel as though you are disconnected from your own body or as if you are watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I'm definitely in my own body. I'm just... frozen, I guess. But I'm not watching myself from the outside or feeling disconnected or anything. I'm right there, just stuck on the numbers.\n\nDoctor: When you are in one of these 'stuck' states, can you respond if someone talks to you or asks you a question, or are you unable to communicate until the episode passes?\n\nPatient: No, I can't really talk to anyone when it happens. That's what actually scared everyone at work. I would just freeze up right in the middle of a dinner service. Or I'd start doing things completely out of order... like, I'd just be standing over by the soda station, holding the key to the cash drawer, and I'd just keep repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out my checks. If someone tried to ask me a question, I couldn't really answer them until it was over.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you are unable to respond to others, do you feel like you are in a dream-like state or that your consciousness is clouded in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it feels like a dream. It's not... cloudy or anything like that. My head is actually really clear, but it's just completely filled up with the numbers. I'm awake and I know where I am, I just can't snap out of it.\n\nDoctor: When you are stuck in these episodes, have your coworkers mentioned if your eyes look strange, such as fluttering, staring blankly without blinking, or if your face seems to change color?\n\nPatient: No, nobody has ever said anything like that to me. They just said I was standing there repeating the numbers. They haven't mentioned my eyes doing anything weird or my face changing color. Just that I wouldn't answer them and I was stuck.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It's not... cloudy or anything like that. My head is actually really clear, but it's just completely filled up with the numbers. I'm awake and I know where I am, I just can't snap out of it.\n\nDoctor: When you are stuck in these episodes, have your coworkers mentioned if your eyes look strange, such as fluttering, staring blankly without blinking, or if your face seems to change color?\n\nPatient: No, nobody has ever said anything like that to me. They just said I was standing there repeating the numbers. They haven't mentioned my eyes doing anything weird or my face changing color. Just that I wouldn't answer them and I was stuck.\n\nDoctor: Are you aware of any specific emotional triggers, such as feeling overwhelmed, stressed, or angry, that occur immediately before these episodes start?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I'm not... I don't pick at my clothes or smack my lips or anything. I really just stand there still while I'm stuck on the numbers. So, no, no twitching or weird movements like that.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you feel 'stuck,' do you feel as though you are disconnected from your own body or as if you are watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I'm definitely in my own body. I'm just... frozen, I guess. But I'm not watching myself from the outside or feeling disconnected or anything. It's always just me. I don't feel like a different person or some other version of myself is taking over. I'm just... stuck in my own head, repeating those numbers. But it's definitely still me the whole time.\n\nDoctor: After the episode finally passes and you are able to move or speak again, do you feel confused, exhausted, or like you need to sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like, the other day I just ended up standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out the checks. So, yeah, there's no gap in my memory. I just kind of get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes start, do you notice any strange physical sensations, like an unusual smell, a rising feeling in your stomach, or a sudden sense of déjà vu?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't smelled anything weird or felt anything like that in my stomach. And it's definitely not déjà vu. It just kind of happens, without any warning signs like that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of these specific episodes at work, do you find yourself feeling constantly worried or anxious about various aspects of your life, like your health, finances, or other daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, not really.
